\title{The group $G(\mathfrak m)$ is perfect: a machine-checked proof}
\date{}
\begin{document}
\maketitle
\begin{abstract}
We present a formal verification of Theorem 3.3 of the source. The theorem states that the group $G(\mathfrak m)$ is perfect, where $G(\mathfrak m)$ is given by generators $H_1(s),H_2(s),X_{-1}(u),Y_{-1}(u),X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and the relations (H), (Re), (Im), (U). In the formalization the coefficient function $c$ of the modular function $J$ is replaced by an arbitrary function $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, and the only hypothesis is $c(2)\ge 1$. The group is built as a presented group, the free group modulo the normal closure of the relators. The formal theorem states that its commutator subgroup is the whole group. The proof follows the source: it shows that each generator is a product of commutators, using (U:3a), (U:3b), (Im:1), (Im:6b), (Im:6d), (Re:3), (Im:3), (H:1a) and (Re:5a).
\end{abstract}

\tableofcontents

\section{Statement}

Let $c(j)$ be the coefficient of $q^j$ in the modular function
\[
J(q)=\sum_{j\ge -1}c(j)q^j=\frac1q+196884\,q+21493760\,q^2+864299970\,q^3+\cdots,
\]
so that $c(-1)=1$, $c(0)=0$ and $c(1)=196884$. Define the index set
\[
E=\{(\ell,j,k)\mid j\in\mathbb N,\ 1\le k\le c(j),\ 0\le \ell<j\}.
\]
For $0\le \ell<j$ put
\[
c_{\ell j}:=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)\in\mathbb C.
\]
Let $\mathcal X$ be the set of symbols $H_1(s)$, $H_2(s)$, $X_{-1}(u)$, $Y_{-1}(u)$, $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$, $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$, where $s\in\Cx$, $u\in\mathbb C$ and $(\ell,j,k)\in E$. Let $F(\mathcal X)$ be the free group on $\mathcal X$. The commutator is $(a,b):=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$.

The relations $\mathcal R$ below are imposed for all $s,t\in\Cx$, $u,v\in\mathbb C$ and $(\ell,j,k),(m,p,q)\in E$.
\begin{align*}
\tag{Re:0} \wt_{-1}(s)&:=X_{-1}(s)Y_{-1}(-s^{-1})X_{-1}(s),\qquad \wt_{-1}:=\wt_{-1}(1),\\
\tag{Im:0} \wt_{\ell,jk}(s)&:=X_{\ell,jk}(s)\,Y_{\ell,jk}\!\left(\tfrac{-s^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\right)X_{\ell,jk}(s),\qquad \wt_{\ell,jk}:=\wt_{\ell,jk}(1),\\
\tag{H:1a} H_1(s)H_1(t)&=H_1(st),\\
\tag{H:1b} H_2(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(st),\\
\tag{H:2} H_1(s)H_2(t)&=H_2(t)H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:1a} X_{-1}(u)X_{-1}(v)&=X_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:1b} Y_{-1}(u)Y_{-1}(v)&=Y_{-1}(u+v),\\
\tag{Re:2} Y_{-1}(-t)X_{-1}(s)Y_{-1}(t)&=X_{-1}(-t^{-1})Y_{-1}(-t^2s)X_{-1}(t^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:3} \wt_{-1}(s)\wt_{-1}&=H_1(-s)H_2(-s^{-1}),\\
\tag{Re:4a} \wt_{-1}X_{-1}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:4b} \wt_{-1}Y_{-1}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{-1}(-u),\\
\tag{Re:5a} \wt_{-1}H_1(s)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=H_2(s),\\
\tag{Re:5b} \wt_{-1}H_2(s)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=H_1(s),\\
\tag{Re:6a} H_1(s)X_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Re:6b} H_2(s)X_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6c} H_1(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(s^{-1}u),\\
\tag{Re:6d} H_2(s)Y_{-1}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{-1}(su),\\
\tag{Im:1a} X_{\ell,jk}(u)X_{\ell,jk}(v)&=X_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:1b} Y_{\ell,jk}(u)Y_{\ell,jk}(v)&=Y_{\ell,jk}(u+v),\\
\tag{Im:2} Y_{\ell,jk}(-t)X_{\ell,jk}(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(t)&=X_{\ell,jk}\!\left(\tfrac{-t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\right)Y_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}t^2s)X_{\ell,jk}\!\left(\tfrac{t^{-1}}{c_{\ell j}}\right),\\
\tag{Im:3} \wt_{\ell,jk}\bigl(s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}\bigr)\wt_{\ell,jk}&=H_1\bigl((-s)^{j-\ell}\bigr)H_2\bigl((-s)^{\ell+1}\bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4a} \wt_{\ell,jk}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}\bigl(-u/c_{\ell j}\bigr),\\
\tag{Im:4b} \wt_{\ell,jk}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(-c_{\ell j}u),\\
\tag{Im:5a} \wt_{\ell,jk}H_1(s^{j-\ell})\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_2(s^{-(\ell+1)}),\\
\tag{Im:5b} \wt_{\ell,jk}H_2(s^{\ell+1})\wt_{\ell,jk}^{-1}&=H_1(s^{-(j-\ell)}),\\
\tag{Im:6a} H_1(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{\ell+1}u),\\
\tag{Im:6b} H_2(s)X_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}u),\\
\tag{Im:6c} H_1(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_1(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(\ell+1)}u),\\
\tag{Im:6d} H_2(s)Y_{\ell,jk}(u)H_2(s)^{-1}&=Y_{\ell,jk}(s^{-(j-\ell)}u),\\
\tag{U:1a} (X_{-1}(u),X_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1b} (Y_{-1}(u),Y_{j-1,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1c} (Y_{-1}(u),X_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:1d} (X_{-1}(u),Y_{0,jk}(v))&=1,\\
\tag{U:2} (X_{\ell,jk}(u),Y_{m,pq}(v))&=1\quad\text{if } j\ne p \text{ or } k\ne q \text{ or } |\ell-m|>1,\\
\tag{U:3a} (X_{1,2k}(u),Y_{0,2k}(v))&=X_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:3b} (X_{0,2k}(u),Y_{1,2k}(v))&=Y_{-1}(uv),\\
\tag{U:4a} \wt_{-1}X_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=X_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr),\\
\tag{U:4b} \wt_{-1}Y_{\ell,jk}(u)\wt_{-1}^{-1}&=Y_{j-1-\ell,jk}\bigl((-1)^{j-\ell-1}u\bigr).
\end{align*}
Define $G(\mathfrak m)=\langle\mathcal X\mid\mathcal R\rangle=F(\mathcal X)/N_{\mathcal R}$, where $N_{\mathcal R}$ is the normal closure of $\mathcal R$.

\begin{theorem}[Theorem 3.3 of the source]
The group $G(\mathfrak m)$ is perfect, that is, $G(\mathfrak m)=(G(\mathfrak m),G(\mathfrak m))$.
\end{theorem}

\section{Formalization}

Throughout, $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ is an arbitrary function and $\mathbb N=\{0,1,2,\dots\}$.

\begin{definition}[Index set]
For $\ell,j,k\in\mathbb N$, say that $(\ell,j,k)$ is \emph{valid} if $\ell<j$, $1\le k$ and $k\le c(j)$. The set of valid triples is exactly the set $E$ of the source, built from the given $c$. Since $\ell<j$ forces $j\ge 1$, the values $c(-1)$ and $c(0)$ play no role.
\end{definition}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Valid}}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ Index set E: (ℓ,j,k) with 0 ≤ ℓ \PYZlt{} j and 1 ≤ k ≤ c(j). }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}
\PY{k+kn}{def}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Valid}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Prop}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZlt{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{∧}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\begin{definition}[Generators]
The set $\mathcal X_c$ consists of the symbols $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\Cx$, $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb C$, and $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb C$ and valid $(\ell,j,k)$.

Formally, the last two kinds carry a proof of validity. By proof irrelevance, any two such proofs give the same symbol, so $\mathcal X_c$ is in natural bijection with the source's $\mathcal X$.
\end{definition}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{Gen}}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{l+s+sd}{/\PYZhy{}\PYZhy{}}\PY{l+s+sd}{ The generating symbols. }\PY{l+s+sd}{\PYZhy{}/}

\begin{definition}
$F_c:=F(\mathcal X_c)$ is the free group on $\mathcal X_c$.
\end{definition}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{F}}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{abbrev}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{F}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{FreeGroup}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Gen}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\begin{definition}
The following maps send a parameter to the corresponding free generator of $F_c$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $s\mapsto H_1(s)$ for $s\in\Cx$;
\item $s\mapsto H_2(s)$ for $s\in\Cx$;
\item $u\mapsto X_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb C$;
\item $u\mapsto Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb C$;
\item $u\mapsto X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for valid $(\ell,j,k)$ and $u\in\mathbb C$;
\item $u\mapsto Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for valid $(\ell,j,k)$ and $u\in\mathbb C$.
\end{itemize}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}
For $\ell,j\in\mathbb N$,
\[
c_{\ell j}:=(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j\mathbin{\dot-}1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j\mathbin{\dot-}\ell)\in\mathbb C,
\]
where $\dot-$ is truncated subtraction on $\mathbb N$. For valid triples we have $\ell<j$, so this agrees with the source's $c_{\ell j}$. In particular $c_{\ell j}\neq 0$ there.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}
For $a,b\in F_c$:
\begin{itemize}
\item the commutator is $(a,b):=aba^{-1}b^{-1}$;
\item the relator of the equation $a=b$ is $\rho(a,b):=ab^{-1}$.
\end{itemize}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}
For $s\in\Cx$ and valid $(\ell,j,k)$, define elements of $F_c$:
\[
\wt_{-1}(s):=X_{-1}(s)\,Y_{-1}(-s^{-1})\,X_{-1}(s),
\qquad
\wt_{\ell,jk}(s):=X_{\ell,jk}(s)\,Y_{\ell,jk}\bigl(-s^{-1}/c_{\ell j}\bigr)\,X_{\ell,jk}(s).
\]
These are (Re:0) and (Im:0). Write $\wt_{-1}:=\wt_{-1}(1)$ and $\wt_{\ell,jk}:=\wt_{\ell,jk}(1)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Relators]
$\mathcal R_c\subseteq F_c$ is the set of the following elements.
\begin{itemize}
\item For every relation of the source written as an equation $a=b$, the relator $\rho(a,b)$. These are (H:1a), (H:1b), (H:2), (Re:1a)--(Re:6d), (Im:1a)--(Im:6d), (U:3a), (U:3b), (U:4a), (U:4b).
\item For every relation of the form $(a,b)=1$, the relator $(a,b)$. These are (U:1a)--(U:1d) and (U:2).
\end{itemize}
The parameters range exactly as in the source: $s,t\in\Cx$, $u,v\in\mathbb C$, all triples valid. Further remarks:
\begin{itemize}
\item In (U:1a) and (U:1b) the triple $(j-1,j,k)$ is required to be valid; in (U:1c) and (U:1d), the triple $(0,j,k)$.
\item In (U:2) the condition reads $j\ne p$ or $k\ne q$ or $|\ell-m|>1$, with $|\ell-m|$ computed in $\mathbb Z$.
\item In (U:3a) and (U:3b) both $(0,2,k)$ and $(1,2,k)$ must be valid.
\item In (U:4a) and (U:4b) the target triple $(j-1-\ell,j,k)$ is also required to be valid. This is automatic for valid $(\ell,j,k)$.
\item Negative exponents are written as inverses of positive powers, e.g.\ $H_2((s^{\ell+1})^{-1})$ in (Im:5a). This is the same element.
\end{itemize}
Each relator is written out explicitly in $F_c$, with $c_{\ell j}$, $\wt_{-1}$ and $\wt_{\ell,jk}$ as above. A check relation by relation shows that $\mathcal R_c$ is exactly the source's $\mathcal R$ (built from $c$).
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}
$G_c:=F_c/N_{\mathcal R_c}$, where $N_{\mathcal R_c}$ is the normal closure of $\mathcal R_c$. Let $\pi:F_c\to G_c$ be the quotient homomorphism.
\end{definition}

\PY{k+kn}{abbrev}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Grp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k+kt}{Type}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{Rels}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

\paragraph{The target statement.}
For a group $G$, $(G,G)$ denotes the commutator subgroup, i.e.\ the subgroup generated by all $(g,h)=ghg^{-1}h^{-1}$. This is the same convention as in the source.

\begin{leanblock}{Lean: target statement \texttt{gsimple\_g\_m\_perfect}, fixed before the proof; \texttt{solution} is checked to have exactly this type}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{gsimple\PYZus{}g\PYZus{}m\PYZus{}perfect}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{hc}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{≤}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{2}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{commutator}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Grp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{⊤}\PY{+w}{ }
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}

The target statement, proved as \leanname{solution} below, says:
\begin{quote}
For every $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge 1$, $(G_c,G_c)=G_c$.
\end{quote}

Relation to the source:
\begin{itemize}
\item The source's group $G(\mathfrak m)$ is $G_c$ for $c$ equal to the coefficients of $J$ restricted to $\mathbb N$. That function satisfies $c(2)=21493760\ge1$.
\item The modular function $J$ is not formalized. Accordingly, the value $c(2)=21493760$ is a fact from the source, not something checked formally.
\item The formal theorem is more general than the source: it holds for every $c$ with $c(2)\ge 1$. As the problem set notes, the proof uses $c$ only through $E$ and through the existence of $(0,2,k),(1,2,k)\in E$ for some $k$. The formal proof takes $k=1$.
\end{itemize}

\section{Proof}

\subsection{Overview}

The argument shows that $(G_c,G_c)$ contains the image of every generator, so it equals $G_c$. The lemmas map to the steps of the source proof as follows.
\begin{itemize}
\item Bookkeeping in the presented group: \cref{lem:mk_rel_one,lem:mk_eqr,lem:subgroup_top_of_gens,lem:cm_mem_commutator}.
\item $X_{-1},Y_{-1}$ via (U:3a) and (U:3b): \cref{lem:xm_mem,lem:ym_mem}.
\item $X_{\ell,jk}$ via (Im:1a) and (Im:6b): \cref{lem:exists_unit_pow_ne_one,lem:xx_add_mk,lem:xx_sub_mk,lem:xx_conj_mem,lem:xx_mem}.
\item $Y_{\ell,jk}$ via (Im:1b) and (Im:6d): \cref{lem:yy_add_mk,lem:yy_sub_mk,lem:yy_conj_mem_aiprover_s0,lem:yy_conj_mem,lem:yy_mem}.
\item The elements $\wt$: \cref{lem:wm_mem,lem:ww_mem}.
\item $H_1$ via (Re:3), (Im:3) with $(\ell,j)=(0,2)$, and (H:1a): \cref{lem:h1h2_neg_mem,lem:h1h2_inv_mem,lem:im3_mem,lem:h1sq_h2_mem,lem:h1_cube_eq,lem:exists_unit_cube,lem:h1_mem}.
\item $H_2$ via (Re:5a): \cref{lem:h2_eq_conj,lem:h2_mem}.
\end{itemize}

The formal proof differs from the source in the following points.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{$X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$.} Instead of factoring $z=uv$, the proof uses (U:3a) and (U:3b) with $v=1$ and $k=1$. The source's step for $Y_{-1}$ contains a typographical slip ("$Y_{-1}(uv)(X_{0,2k}(u),Y_{1,2k}(v))$"); the formal step uses the relation (U:3b) as stated.
\item \emph{Choice of $s$.} The element $s$ with $s^{j-\ell}\ne1$ is taken to be $s=2$. For $Y_{\ell,jk}$ the multiplier is $(s^{j-\ell})^{-1}\neq1$, exactly as in the source.
\item \emph{The step $H_1(z^3)$.} The source uses $H_1(z^{-2})^{-1}=H_1(z^2)$ and $H_1(z)H_1(z^2)=H_1(z^3)$. Formally, (H:1a) is first used to show that $s\mapsto\pi(H_1(s))$ is a homomorphism $\Cx\to G_c$. The $H_2$-factors cancel by group arithmetic alone; (H:2) is not needed.
\item \emph{The step $H_2(s)$.} It uses that $(G_c,G_c)$ is normal. Membership $\wt_{-1}\in(G_c,G_c)$ is not needed for this step.
\item \emph{Unused lemmas.} \cref{lem:mk_rel_one,lem:yy_add_mk,lem:h1h2_neg_mem,lem:im3_mem} are proved but not used in the final chain. Instead, \cref{lem:h1h2_inv_mem,lem:h1sq_h2_mem} apply (Re:3) and (Im:3) directly with the substituted parameter.
\end{enumerate}
The hypothesis $c(2)\ge1$ is used only to make $(0,2,1)$ and $(1,2,1)$ valid.

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{mk_rel_one}]\label{lem:mk_rel_one}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ and $r\in F_c$. If $r\in\mathcal R_c$, then $\pi(r)=1$ in $G_c$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{mk\_rel\_one} --- proved by AIProver, job 1, session 1}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]
\PY{k+kn}{theorem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{mk\PYZus{}rel\PYZus{}one}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{→}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℕ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{r}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{F}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Rel}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{r}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}
\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Rels}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{r}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{l+m+mi}{1}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{apply}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{one\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}mem}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Every relator lies in the normal closure $N_{\mathcal R_c}$, which is the kernel of $\pi$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{mk_eqr}]\label{lem:mk_eqr}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ and $a,b\in F_c$. If $\rho(a,b)=ab^{-1}\in\mathcal R_c$, then $\pi(a)=\pi(b)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{leanblock}{Lean: \texttt{mk\_eqr} --- proved by AIProver, job 1, session 1}
\begin{Verbatim}[commandchars=\\\{\},breaklines=true]

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Rels}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{a}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Rels}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{b}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:=}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{k}{by}
\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mk\PYZus{}eq\PYZus{}mk\PYZus{}of\PYZus{}mul\PYZus{}inv\PYZus{}mem}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{o}{)}

\begin{proof}
We have $ab^{-1}\in\mathcal R_c\subseteq\ker\pi$. Hence $\pi(a)\pi(b)^{-1}=1$, i.e.\ $\pi(a)=\pi(b)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens}]\label{lem:subgroup_top_of_gens}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ and let $N$ be a subgroup of $G_c$. If $\pi(x)\in N$ for every $x\in\mathcal X_c$, then $N=G_c$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The subgroup generated by $\pi(\mathcal X_c)$ is all of $G_c$, since $\pi$ is surjective and $F_c$ is generated by $\mathcal X_c$. This generated subgroup is contained in $N$ by hypothesis.
\end{proof}

\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h\PYZus{}cm\PYZus{}mem}

\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
Since $c(2)\ge1$, the triples $(1,2,1)$ and $(0,2,1)$ are valid. Relation (U:3a) with $k=1$ and $v=1$ gives
\[
\pi\bigl((X_{1,21}(u),Y_{0,21}(1))\bigr)=\pi(X_{-1}(u\cdot1))=\pi(X_{-1}(u))
\]
by \cref{lem:mk_eqr}. The left-hand side is the commutator of $\pi(X_{1,21}(u))$ and $\pi(Y_{0,21}(1))$, so it lies in $\CG$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{ym_mem}]\label{lem:ym_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge1$. Then for every $u\in\mathbb C$, $\pi(Y_{-1}(u))\in\CG$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
As in \cref{lem:xm_mem}, $(0,2,1)$ and $(1,2,1)$ are valid. Relation (U:3b) with $k=1$ and $v=1$, together with \cref{lem:mk_eqr}, gives
\[
\pi\bigl((X_{0,21}(u),Y_{1,21}(1))\bigr)=\pi(Y_{-1}(u)).
\]
The left-hand side lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:cm_mem_commutator}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{wm_mem}]\label{lem:wm_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge1$. Then for every $s\in\Cx$, $\pi(\wt_{-1}(s))\in\CG$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We have $\pi(\wt_{-1}(s))=\pi(X_{-1}(s))\,\pi(Y_{-1}(-s^{-1}))\,\pi(X_{-1}(s))$. Each factor lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:xm_mem,lem:ym_mem}, and $\CG$ is a subgroup.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{exists_unit_pow_ne_one}]\label{lem:exists_unit_pow_ne_one}
For every $d\in\mathbb N$ with $d>0$ there is $s\in\Cx$ with $s^d\ne1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Take $s=2$. If $2^d=1$, then taking squared absolute values gives $4^d=1$. But $4^d>1$ for $d>0$, a contradiction.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{xx_add_mk}]\label{lem:xx_add_mk}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid and $u,v\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(u+v))=\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(u))\,\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(v)).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{yy_add_mk}]\label{lem:yy_add_mk}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid and $u,v\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(u+v))=\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(u))\,\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(v)).
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{xx_sub_mk}]\label{lem:xx_sub_mk}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid and $a,b\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(a-b))=\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(a))\,\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(b))^{-1}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $X=X_{\ell,jk}$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The relator (Im:1a) with $u=v=0$ is $X(0)X(0)X(0)^{-1}$, which equals $X(0)$ in $F_c$. Hence $\pi(X(0))=1$.
\item By \cref{lem:xx_add_mk}, $\pi(X(u))\pi(X(-u))=\pi(X(0))=1$, so $\pi(X(-u))=\pi(X(u))^{-1}$.
\item Therefore $\pi(X(a-b))=\pi(X(a+(-b)))=\pi(X(a))\pi(X(-b))=\pi(X(a))\pi(X(b))^{-1}$, again by \cref{lem:xx_add_mk}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{yy_sub_mk}]\label{lem:yy_sub_mk}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid and $a,b\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(a-b))=\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(a))\,\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(b))^{-1}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Relation (Im:1b) with parameters $a-b$ and $b$, together with \cref{lem:mk_eqr}, gives
\[
\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(a-b))\,\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(b))=\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(a)).
\]
Multiply on the right by $\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(b))^{-1}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{xx_conj_mem}]\label{lem:xx_conj_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid, $s\in\Cx$ and $v\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi\bigl(X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}v)\bigr)\,\pi\bigl(X_{\ell,jk}(v)\bigr)^{-1}\in\CG.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By (Im:6b) and \cref{lem:mk_eqr},
\[
\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(s^{j-\ell}v))=\pi(H_2(s))\,\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(v))\,\pi(H_2(s))^{-1}.
\]
Hence the element in question is the commutator $\bigl(\pi(H_2(s)),\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(v))\bigr)$, which lies in $\CG$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{yy_conj_mem_aiprover_s0}]\label{lem:yy_conj_mem_aiprover_s0}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid, $s\in\Cx$ and $v\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi\bigl(Y_{\ell,jk}((s^{j-\ell})^{-1}v)\bigr)\,\pi\bigl(Y_{\ell,jk}(v)\bigr)^{-1}\in\CG.
\]
\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{PresentedGroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Rels}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{yy}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{GM}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{Grp}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{Subgroup}\PY{n+nb+bp}{.}\PY{n}{mem\PYZus{}top}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZus{}}\PY{o}{,}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{rfl}\PY{n+nb+bp}{⟩}
\end{Verbatim}
\end{leanblock}
\begin{proof}
By (Im:6d) and \cref{lem:mk_eqr},
\[
\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}((s^{j-\ell})^{-1}v))=\pi(H_2(s))\,\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(v))\,\pi(H_2(s))^{-1}.
\]
So the element is the commutator $\bigl(\pi(H_2(s)),\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(v))\bigr)\in\CG$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{yy_conj_mem}]\label{lem:yy_conj_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid, $s\in\Cx$ and $v\in\mathbb C$. Then
\[
\pi\bigl(Y_{\ell,jk}((s^{j-\ell})^{-1}v)\bigr)\,\pi\bigl(Y_{\ell,jk}(v)\bigr)^{-1}\in\CG.
\]
\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{    }\PY{n}{rw}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{[}\PY{n}{xx\PYZus{}sub\PYZus{}mk}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{c}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{k}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{h}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{s}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{:}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{ℂ}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZca{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{o}{(}\PY{n}{j}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{\PYZhy{}}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{l}\PY{o}{)}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n+nb+bp}{*}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{)}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{v}\PY{o}{]}

\begin{proof}
Validity gives $\ell<j$, so $d:=j-\ell>0$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item By \cref{lem:exists_unit_pow_ne_one} choose $s\in\Cx$ with $s^d\ne1$.
\item Put $v:=u/(s^d-1)$. Then $u=(s^d-1)v=s^dv-v$.
\item By \cref{lem:xx_sub_mk}, $\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(u))=\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(s^dv))\,\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(v))^{-1}$.
\item This lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:xx_conj_mem}.
\end{enumerate}
No hypothesis on $c(2)$ is needed.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{yy_mem}]\label{lem:yy_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid and $u\in\mathbb C$. Then $\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(u))\in\CG$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Let $d:=j-\ell>0$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item By \cref{lem:exists_unit_pow_ne_one} choose $s\in\Cx$ with $s^d\ne1$. Then $t:=(s^d)^{-1}\ne1$.
\item Put $v:=u/(t-1)$, so that $tv-v=u$.
\item By \cref{lem:yy_sub_mk}, $\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(u))=\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(tv))\,\pi(Y_{\ell,jk}(v))^{-1}$.
\item This lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:yy_conj_mem}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
We have
\[
\pi(\wt_{\ell,jk}(s))=\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(s))\,\pi\bigl(Y_{\ell,jk}(-s^{-1}/c_{\ell j})\bigr)\,\pi(X_{\ell,jk}(s)).
\]
Each factor lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:xx_mem,lem:yy_mem}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h1h2_neg_mem}]\label{lem:h1h2_neg_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge1$, and $s\in\Cx$. Then $\pi\bigl(H_1(-s)H_2(-s^{-1})\bigr)\in\CG$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}

\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
Relation (Re:3) with $s=-x$ reads
\[
\wt_{-1}(-x)\,\wt_{-1}=H_1(x)H_2(x^{-1}),
\]
since $-(-x)=x$ and $-((-x)^{-1})=x^{-1}$. By \cref{lem:mk_eqr}, $\pi(H_1(x)H_2(x^{-1}))=\pi(\wt_{-1}(-x))\,\pi(\wt_{-1}(1))$. This lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:wm_mem}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{im3_mem}]\label{lem:im3_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$, $(\ell,j,k)$ valid and $s\in\Cx$. Then
\[
\pi\bigl(H_1((-s)^{j-\ell})\,H_2((-s)^{\ell+1})\bigr)\in\CG.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By (Im:3) and \cref{lem:mk_eqr}, the element equals $\pi(\wt_{\ell,jk}(s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}))\,\pi(\wt_{\ell,jk}(1))$. Both factors lie in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:ww_mem}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h1sq_h2_mem}]\label{lem:h1sq_h2_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge1$, and $y\in\Cx$. Then $\pi\bigl(H_1(y^2)H_2(y)\bigr)\in\CG$.
\end{lemma}

\PY{+w}{  }\PY{n}{exact}\PY{+w}{ }\PY{n}{hprod}

\begin{proof}
Since $c(2)\ge1$, the triple $(0,2,1)$ is valid. Relation (Im:3) for $(\ell,j,k)=(0,2,1)$ and parameter $s=-y$ reads
\[
\wt_{0,21}(y^2)\,\wt_{0,21}=H_1(y^2)H_2(y),
\]
because $(-y)^{(0+1)(2-0)}=y^2$, $(-(-y))^{2}=y^2$ and $(-(-y))^{1}=y$. By \cref{lem:mk_eqr}, $\pi(H_1(y^2)H_2(y))=\pi(\wt_{0,21}(y^2))\,\pi(\wt_{0,21}(1))$. This lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:ww_mem}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h1_cube_eq}]\label{lem:h1_cube_eq}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ and $z\in\Cx$. Then in $G_c$,
\[
\pi\bigl(H_1(z^3)\bigr)=\pi\bigl(H_1(z)H_2(z^{-1})\bigr)\cdot\pi\bigl(H_1((z^{-1})^2)H_2(z^{-1})\bigr)^{-1}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Write $\eta(s):=\pi(H_1(s))$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item By (H:1a) and \cref{lem:mk_eqr}, $\eta(s)\eta(t)=\eta(st)$ for all $s,t\in\Cx$.
\item With $s=t=1$ this gives $\eta(1)^2=\eta(1)$, hence $\eta(1)=1$.
\item With $t=s^{-1}$ it gives $\eta(s)\eta(s^{-1})=1$, hence $\eta(s^{-1})=\eta(s)^{-1}$.
\item By induction on $n$, $\eta(x^n)=\eta(x)^n$ for all $n\in\mathbb N$.
\end{enumerate}
Put $a:=\eta(z)$ and $b:=\pi(H_2(z^{-1}))$. Then $\eta((z^{-1})^2)=(a^{-1})^2$, and the right-hand side equals
\[
ab\,\bigl(a^{-2}b\bigr)^{-1}=ab\,b^{-1}a^{2}=a^3=\eta(z^3).
\]
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{exists_unit_cube}]\label{lem:exists_unit_cube}
For every $s\in\Cx$ there is $z\in\Cx$ with $z^3=s$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $\mathbb C$ is algebraically closed, there is $w\in\mathbb C$ with $w^3=s$. Since $s\ne0$, also $w\ne0$, so $w\in\Cx$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}
By \cref{lem:exists_unit_cube} choose $z\in\Cx$ with $z^3=s$. Then:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\pi(H_1(z)H_2(z^{-1}))\in\CG$ by \cref{lem:h1h2_inv_mem};
\item $\pi(H_1((z^{-1})^2)H_2(z^{-1}))\in\CG$ by \cref{lem:h1sq_h2_mem} with $y=z^{-1}$.
\end{itemize}
Since $\CG$ is a subgroup, the first element times the inverse of the second lies in $\CG$. By \cref{lem:h1_cube_eq}, this product equals $\pi(H_1(z^3))=\pi(H_1(s))$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h2_eq_conj}]\label{lem:h2_eq_conj}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ and $s\in\Cx$. Then
\[
\pi(H_2(s))=\pi(\wt_{-1})\,\pi(H_1(s))\,\pi(\wt_{-1})^{-1}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[\leanname{h2_mem}]\label{lem:h2_mem}
Let $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge1$, and $s\in\Cx$. Then $\pi(H_2(s))\in\CG$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By \cref{lem:h2_eq_conj}, $\pi(H_2(s))$ is a conjugate of $\pi(H_1(s))$, which lies in $\CG$ by \cref{lem:h1_mem}. The commutator subgroup is normal, so the conjugate also lies in $\CG$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}
For every $c:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N$ with $c(2)\ge1$, $\CG=G_c$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \cref{lem:subgroup_top_of_gens} it suffices to show that $\pi(x)\in\CG$ for every generator $x\in\mathcal X_c$. We check each kind of generator:
\begin{itemize}
\item $x=H_1(s)$: \cref{lem:h1_mem};
\item $x=H_2(s)$: \cref{lem:h2_mem};
\item $x=X_{-1}(u)$: \cref{lem:xm_mem};
\item $x=Y_{-1}(u)$: \cref{lem:ym_mem};
\item $x=X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ with $(\ell,j,k)$ valid: \cref{lem:xx_mem};
\item $x=Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ with $(\ell,j,k)$ valid: \cref{lem:yy_mem}.
\end{itemize}
For $c$ the sequence of coefficients of $J$, we have $c(2)=21493760\ge1$, which gives the source's Theorem 3.3. This value is taken from the source and is not checked formally.
\end{proof}

\section{Independent review}

A referee model (claude-opus-5-5), which took no part in the formalization or the proof, compared the verified Lean file with the source. Verdict: \textbf{FAITHFUL}.

\paragraph{Definitions.} \leanname{GM.Valid} c l j k is $l<j \wedge 1\le k \wedge k\le c(j)$. This is exactly membership of $(\ell,j,k)$ in $E$, with $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ taken as a parameter, as the formalization note permits. For $j=0$ the condition $l<0$ is false, so no spurious indices appear. Because the proof argument is a \texttt{Prop}, proof irrelevance means \leanname{Gen.X} and \leanname{Gen.Y} create no duplicate generators.

\leanname{GM.Gen} gives exactly the symbols of $\mathcal{X}$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $H_1(s)$ and $H_2(s)$ for $s\in\mathbb{C}^\times$;
\item $X_{-1}(u)$ and $Y_{-1}(u)$ for $u\in\mathbb{C}$;
\item $X_{\ell,jk}(u)$ and $Y_{\ell,jk}(u)$ for $(\ell,j,k)\in E$.
\end{itemize}
\leanname{GM.F} is the free group on these symbols. \leanname{GM.Grp} is \leanname{PresentedGroup} of \leanname{GM.Rels}, which is $F(\mathcal{X})/N_{\mathcal{R}}$.

\leanname{GM.cc} is $(-1)^{\ell+1}\binom{j-1}{\ell}(\ell+1)(j-\ell)$. Truncated subtraction is harmless because $\ell<j$. On $E$ the value is nonzero, so the divisions by $c_{\ell j}$ are genuine.

\leanname{GM.wm} and \leanname{GM.ww} match (Re:0) and (Im:0). \leanname{GM.cm} is $aba^{-1}b^{-1}$, the source convention. \leanname{GM.eqr} encodes $a=b$ as the relator $ab^{-1}$.

I checked every constructor of \leanname{GM.Rel} against the source:
\begin{itemize}
\item (H:1a), (H:1b), (H:2) match.
\item (Re:1a) through (Re:6d) match. In (Re:2) both $s$ and $t$ range over units, as in the source.
\item (Im:1a) through (Im:6d) match. The arguments $-t^{-1}/c_{\ell j}$, $-c_{\ell j}t^2 s$ and $t^{-1}/c_{\ell j}$ are correct. In (Im:3) the exponents are $s^{(\ell+1)(j-\ell)}$, $(-s)^{j-\ell}$ and $(-s)^{\ell+1}$. In (Im:5) and (Im:6) the inverses are written as $(s^{n})^{-1}$.
\item (U:1a) through (U:1d) use the indices $j-1$ and $0$ with validity hypotheses.
\item (U:2) has the disjunction $j\ne p$, $k\ne q$, or $|\ell-m|>1$.
\item (U:3a) and (U:3b) match.
\item (U:4a) and (U:4b) use index $j-1-\ell$ and sign $(-1)^{j-\ell-1}$. The extra validity hypothesis on $j-1-\ell$ is always satisfiable when $(\ell,j,k)\in E$, so no instance is lost.
\end{itemize}
I found no discrepancy.

\paragraph{Statement.} The target is \leanname{gsimple_g_m_perfect}: for every $c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}$ with $1\le c(2)$, the commutator subgroup of \leanname{GM.Grp} $c$ is $\top$. This is the statement $G(\mathfrak m)=(G(\mathfrak m),G(\mathfrak m))$.

The only hypothesis is $c(2)\ge 1$, which the formalization note explicitly allows. The true coefficients of $J$ satisfy it, since $c(2)=21493760$. To recover the source statement one supplies the actual coefficient sequence $c(j)$ for $j\ge 1$. The values $c(0)$ and $c(-1)$ never enter $E$.

The statement is not vacuous. The hypothesis is satisfiable, and the conclusion concerns the group presented by the faithfully transcribed relations.

\paragraph{Proof.} The final step, \leanname{subgroup_top_of_gens}, reduces the theorem to showing that every generator lies in the commutator subgroup. This uses \leanname{PresentedGroup.closure_range_of}. The remaining lemmas mirror the source proof:
\begin{itemize}
\item \leanname{xm_mem} and \leanname{ym_mem} use (U:3a) and (U:3b) with $k=1$ and $v=1$, so $uv=u$. The case $k=1$ is valid because of the hypothesis $c(2)\ge1$.
\item \leanname{xx_mem} and \leanname{yy_mem} use (Im:6b) and (Im:6d) together with (Im:1a) and (Im:1b). The additivity relations are packaged as \leanname{xx_sub_mk} and \leanname{yy_sub_mk}. The proof picks $s=2$ and uses $|2^d|\neq 1$ to get $s^{j-\ell}\ne1$; this is \leanname{exists_unit_pow_ne_one}. It then solves for $v$. This is the same argument as the source.
\item \leanname{wm_mem} and \leanname{ww_mem} correspond to the $\widetilde w$ step of the source.
\item \leanname{h1h2_inv_mem} uses (Re:3) with $s=-x$.
\item \leanname{h1sq_h2_mem} uses (Im:3) with $(\ell,j,k)=(0,2,1)$ and $s=-y$.
\item \leanname{h1_cube_eq} derives the cube identity from (H:1a) alone, as in the source.
\item \leanname{exists_unit_cube} supplies cube roots via algebraic closure. \leanname{h1_mem} then follows.
\item \leanname{h2_mem} uses (Re:5a) and normality of the commutator subgroup. The source instead uses the product of $\widetilde w_{-1}$, $H_1(s)$ and $\widetilde w_{-1}^{-1}$; either argument works.
\end{itemize}
No lemma assumes anything beyond the defining relations and $c(2)\ge1$. Each lemma states exactly the step it represents.

\paragraph{Concerns.} \begin{itemize}
\item The coefficients of $J$ are replaced by an arbitrary $c$ with $c(2)\ge 1$. This is sanctioned by the problem note, and the theorem then applies to the true $c$.
\end{itemize}
